[codex] ## Operating Context
[codex] 
[codex] This repository coordinates the deployment and development environment for the Budget Analyzer application, a reference microservices architecture used as an open source learning resource for AI-assisted development.
[codex] 
[codex] This project is designed for AI-assisted development. The containerized development environment lives in the sibling `../workspace` repository; that repo owns the devcontainer configuration.
[codex] 
[codex] The `ext-authz` Go service implementation lives in the sibling `../ext-authz`
[codex] repository. This orchestration repository owns its Kubernetes manifests, Istio
[codex] `ext_authz` wiring, NGINX integration, Redis ACL wiring, production image
[codex] policy, and deployment documentation.
[codex] 
[codex] The default debugging model is split across host and container:
[codex] - Tilt runs on the host machine
[codex] - AI agents run inside the container
[codex] - The container has shared workspace access and full `kubectl` access to the host-managed cluster
[codex] 
[codex] The agent container is inside the trusted local-development boundary. Local
[codex] workspace files, the local Kind kubeconfig and Kubernetes Secrets, generated
[codex] development TLS keys, and disposable local API-test credentials are not hidden
[codex] from the agent. Never expose staging or production kubeconfigs, cloud or
[codex] deployment credentials, user credentials, or session cookies to this
[codex] container, and never use an agent session to deploy or administer staging or
[codex] production. Before an in-container agent-authorized cluster mutation, require
[codex] the current context and referenced cluster to equal `kind-kind`, require a
[codex] loopback Kubernetes API endpoint, and require the `kind-control-plane` node.
[codex] Use `kind get clusters` only as an additional host-side check because the
[codex] container Docker daemon cannot enumerate the host-managed Kind cluster. For
[codex] the full boundary and API-test target checks, read
[codex] `docs/architecture/autonomous-ai-execution.md` before changing agent sandbox,
[codex] credential, Kubernetes-access, or autonomous execution behavior.
[codex] 
[codex] Production target is Oracle Cloud Infrastructure Free Tier on ARM64:
[codex] - Shape: `VM.Standard.A1.Flex`
[codex] - Capacity: 4 OCPUs, 24 GB RAM
[codex] - OS baseline: Ubuntu 22.04 Minimal
[codex] - Region baseline: Phoenix
[codex] - Deployment model: single-node, production-grade demo environment
[codex] 
[codex] Architecture implications:
[codex] - All container images must support `linux/arm64`
[codex] - Local Tilt/Kind and OCI/k3s are both treated as production-grade deployment paths
[codex] - Observability remains internal-only in both environments
[codex] 
[codex] Architecture principles:
[codex] - Production parity between local Tilt/Kind and OCI/k3s
[codex] - Microservices with clear service boundaries and independent deployability
[codex] - Session-based edge authorization for browser security and session management
[codex] - Istio ingress handles ext_authz and auth-path throttling; NGINX handles API routing, backend/API rate limiting, and load balancing
[codex] - Resource-based routing keeps the frontend decoupled from service topology
[codex] - Defense in depth across Istio ingress, Session Gateway or NGINX, and downstream services
[codex] - Kubernetes-native development uses Tilt live update so code changes reach running pods quickly while the full security stack stays active; see `docs/development/local-environment.md#live-development-pipeline`
[codex] 
[codex] Technology pattern:
[codex] - Frontend: React web application
[codex] - Backends: Spring Boot + Java + Gradle
[codex] - Browser auth edge: Session Gateway (Spring WebFlux)
[codex] - External authorization: `ext-authz` Go service
[codex] - Edge and routing: Istio ingress/egress plus NGINX API gateway
[codex] - Infrastructure: PostgreSQL, Redis, RabbitMQ
[codex] - Monitoring: Prometheus, Grafana, Jaeger, Kiali
[codex] 
[codex] Service topology summary:
[codex] - Frontend: React web application for browser clients
[codex] - Backend workloads: Spring Boot microservices behind NGINX, plus Session Gateway and `ext-authz` on the auth and authorization path
[codex] - Infrastructure: PostgreSQL, Redis, and RabbitMQ in the `infrastructure` namespace; Redis `/data` is PVC-backed through the `redis-data` claim template
[codex] - Monitoring: Prometheus, Grafana, kube-state-metrics, the repo-managed Jaeger v2 backend, and standalone Kiali in `monitoring`; Jaeger and Kiali stay `ClusterIP`-only
[codex] - Ingress and egress: Istio ingress handles browser entry, `ext_authz`, and auth-path throttling; Istio egress stays `ClusterIP` with `REGISTRY_ONLY`
[codex] - Exact service ports and exposure rules live in `docs/architecture/port-reference.md`
[codex] 
[codex] Adding a new service:
[codex] - Create manifests in `kubernetes/services/{name}/`
[codex] - Register the service in `Tiltfile`
[codex] - Add NGINX routes when the service owns public resources
[codex] - See `docs/architecture/session-edge-authorization-pattern.md` and `nginx/README.md`
[codex] 
[codex] Version numbers and concrete dependency selections live in service repos, manifests, and checked-in release inputs, not in this file.
[codex]

[codex] ## Core Runtime Pattern
[codex] 
[codex] The primary request flow is:
[codex] 
[codex] ```text
[codex] Browser -> Istio Ingress -> ext_authz validates session -> NGINX -> services
[codex] Auth paths -> Istio Ingress -> Session Gateway
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Entry-point ownership:
[codex] - Browser auth lifecycle stays on the direct Session Gateway lane
[codex] - API traffic stays on the NGINX lane with ingress-layer `ext_authz`
[codex] - Exact route ownership and `/api-docs` behavior live in `docs/architecture/session-edge-authorization-pattern.md` and `docs-aggregator/README.md`
[codex] 
[codex] Session and identity rules:
[codex] - Session Gateway resolves roles and permissions from `permission-service` during session creation and owns browser session lifecycle on the direct auth lane
[codex] - Keep browser session logic on the direct `/auth/*` lane rather than pushing it through the general API path
[codex] - Exact browser endpoints and the shared Session Gateway/`ext_authz` contract live in `docs/architecture/session-edge-authorization-pattern.md`
[codex] 
[codex] Operational guardrails:
[codex] - Follow the resource-based routing pattern for new API endpoints
[codex] - Treat any new public `/api/...` route as incomplete until both `nginx/nginx.k8s.conf` and `nginx/nginx.production.k8s.conf` claim it and verification proves it does not fall through to the SPA
[codex] - Keep Spring Boot workloads on the startup/readiness/liveness probe pattern used by the checked-in manifests
[codex] - Keep PostgreSQL, RabbitMQ, and Redis on their repo-owned stateful deployment patterns
[codex] - Redis `/data` is PVC-backed. Do not treat pod replacement or `tilt down` as a Redis reset. Use `./scripts/ops/flush-redis.sh` for a logical reset, or recreate the cluster/runtime when a full clean state is required
[codex] 
[codex] Observability rules:
[codex] - Grafana, Prometheus, Jaeger, and Kiali are internal-only in both local Tilt and OCI/k3s
[codex] - Use loopback-only access for operators: raw `kubectl port-forward --address 127.0.0.1 ...` or `./scripts/ops/start-observability-port-forwards.sh`
[codex] - `./scripts/smoketest/verify-observability-port-forward-access.sh` is the focused access proof
[codex] - Do not introduce public observability hostnames
[codex] - Do not use `--address 0.0.0.0` for observability access
[codex] - Exact observability access commands, local ports, and Kiali auth flow live in `docs/architecture/observability.md`
[codex]

[codex] 
[codex] ### Scope

[codex] and canonical dependency-automation documentation.
[codex]

[codex]

[codex] `deploy/scripts/lib/version-contract.sh`, `scripts/lib/pinned-tool-versions.sh`,

[codex] ### Execution steps
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Create `docs/dependency-automation.md` with the policy, cost boundary, bot
[codex]    ownership, activation procedure and failure triage. Transfer active operational
[codex]    ownership there in `docs/OWNERSHIP.md`; link from README and relevant CI docs.
[codex] 2. Add the shared preset and root config. Explicitly configure Kubernetes and
[codex]    Helm-values file patterns; Dockerfile detection alone is insufficient.
[codex] 3. Add documented Renovate annotations/extraction for chart pins in shell and
[codex]    Tilt, image strings in Tilt/scripts, Kind node images, and pinned tool releases.
[codex]    Bind each chart to its real chart name/repository, not just a GitHub project.
[codex]    Cover repeated pins without duplicate native/custom extraction.
[codex] 4. Preserve pinning exceptions, production artifact ownership and checksum
[codex]    contracts. Make stateful/platform/checksum-coupled proposals approval-gated.
[codex] 5. Add a focused workflow using Renovate's official config validator; initialize
[codex]    the coverage report with actual paths and extraction evidence.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Implementation notes
[codex] 
[codex] Use current documented schema options. Validate registry tag formats, K3s build
[codex] suffixes, `v` prefixes and image compatibility suffixes. Do not treat Pod Security
[codex] labels as dependency versions. Existing docs and version-contract comments that
[codex] describe intentional holds should become review prerequisites, not silent ignores.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Validation
[codex] 
[codex] Confirm `node --version` reports major version 24, then run
[codex] `renovate-config-validator` against both files. Validate config against the local
[codex] preset before it is published, then use Renovate's local platform with debug
[codex] extraction/lookup output. Local mode does not implement branch-file mutation and
[codex] downgrades `full` dry runs to lookup; record GitHub-authenticated lookups and a true
[codex] GitHub-platform full dry run as explicit Phase 12 gaps rather than giving an agent a
[codex] token or blocking configuration preparation. Treat Renovate exceptions or error
[codex] logs as failures even if its exit status is zero. Run `bash -n` and `shellcheck` on
[codex] annotated shell files, actionlint on changed workflows, and the existing static
[codex] guardrails when their documented prerequisites are present. Missing local
[codex] prerequisites are blockers, not reasons to bypass checks.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Completion criteria
[codex] 
[codex] Every orchestration row in the coverage contract has extracted identities and
[codex] lookup results or an explicit gap. Config validates and checked-in dependency
[codex] selections remain unchanged. A successful schema check alone is insufficient.
[codex] Publication, authenticated GitHub lookup, published-preset resolution, and hosted
[codex] branch mutation simulation are Phase 12 acceptance work and do not block Phase 1.
[codex]

[codex] ## Project Priorities
[codex] 
[codex] This reference architecture is functionally complete. Current priorities are:
[codex] 1. Documentation improvements and clarifications
[codex] 2. Architectural discussions and pattern explanations
[codex] 3. Bug fixes in existing functionality
[codex] 4. Not net-new features or data-ownership implementation
[codex] 
[codex] ## Shell Script Validation
[codex] 
[codex] When adding or modifying shell scripts in this repo, always validate them before considering the work complete:
[codex] 
[codex] 1. `bash -n &lt;script>`
[codex] 2. `shellcheck &lt;script>`
[codex] 
[codex] Fix all errors and warnings before finishing. Do not suppress a ShellCheck warning without an in-file `# shellcheck disable=SC####` justification comment.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Prerequisites First
[codex] 
[codex] Before implementing a plan or feature:
[codex] 1. Check the relevant documentation for prerequisites
[codex] 2. If prerequisites are missing, stop and call that out
[codex] 3. Do not hack around a missing prerequisite with orchestration-level compensating changes
[codex] 4. Complete prerequisites first, then return to the requested work
[codex] 
[codex] ## Image Pinning And Supply Chain Guardrails
[codex] 
[codex] - Treat checked-in third-party image refs and Dockerfile base images as digest-pinning targets unless an exception is explicitly documented in the executable inventories
[codex] - The executable inventories live in `scripts/lib/image-pinning-targets.txt` and `scripts/lib/allowed-latest-image-refs.txt`
[codex] - Keep those inventories aligned with the static security guardrail checks
[codex] - Only the documented local image repos may remain on `:latest` in checked-in manifests
[codex] - Live Tilt deploys rewrite those same repos to immutable `:tilt-&lt;hash>` refs and currently force `imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent` on those managed deploys
[codex] - Treat every third-party `image:` or `FROM` ref as a digest-pinning target unless it is explicitly excluded in the inventories
[codex] - `tests/setup-flow` and `tests/security-preflight` are stale, non-gating retained assets until they are explicitly realigned to the current Istio-only baseline. Do not treat them as current completion gates
[codex] 
[codex] ## Cross-Repo Boundaries
[codex] 
[codex] Do NOT write code in sibling repositories from this repo context.
[codex] 
[codex] Allowed write surface from this repo:
[codex] - This repository
[codex] - Documentation in sibling repos
[codex] - Configuration in sibling repos when fixing deployment, environment, or manifest wiring issues
[codex] 
[codex] If a fix requires service logic changes in a sibling repo:
[codex] - Stop
[codex] - Describe the needed service-side change clearly
[codex] - Let the user handle it, or switch to that repo's context
[codex] 
[codex] Do not hide sibling-repo contract failures with orchestration workarounds.
[codex] 
[codex] Example: if Spring Boot services fail to expose `/actuator/prometheus` and that contract belongs in `service-common`, fix or rebuild `service-common`. Do not patch orchestration manifests with compensating `MANAGEMENT_ENDPOINTS_WEB_EXPOSURE_INCLUDE=health,prometheus` overrides just to hide the defect.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Production-Grade Parity
[codex] 
[codex] Treat both supported runtime paths, local Tilt/Kind and OCI/k3s, as production-grade microservices deployments.
[codex] 
[codex] Do not present the following as durable fixes:
[codex] - manual live-cluster drift
[codex] - security bypasses
[codex] - relaxed policies
[codex] - untracked Helm overrides
[codex] - degraded persistence such as replacing StatefulSets with `emptyDir`
[codex] - orchestration-level compensations for service-owned defects
[codex] 
[codex] Temporary recovery commands are acceptable only when clearly labeled as diagnostics or incident-recovery steps. Every durable fix must be repo-owned, repeatable from scripts/manifests, documented in the affected runbook or plan, and verified with the relevant smoke or guardrail checks.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Planning Transparency
[codex] 
[codex] Write plans to `docs/plans/`, not hidden locations.
[codex] 
[codex] Plans should be visible, version-controlled collaboration artifacts. Do not hide meaningful project planning in ephemeral or private planning mechanisms.
[codex] 
[codex] When creating an implementation or execution plan intended for AI Session
[codex] Handler, follow the [AI Session Handler plan format](../ai-session-handler/docs/plan-format.md), use
[codex] its canonical template, replace every placeholder, and retain the numbered `## Phase N: Title`
[codex] headings.
[codex] 
[codex] Run a specific plan through the workspace wrapper with:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] ai-session-handler run \
[codex]   --plan /workspace/REPOSITORY/docs/plans/PLAN.md \
[codex]   --max-phases 999 \
[codex]   --quiet \
[codex]   --agent-cmd "/workspace/ai-session-handler/.venv/bin/ai-session-handler-codex-high --model MODEL"
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Omit `--model MODEL` from the quoted agent command to use the wrapper's
[codex] configured or default model.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Autonomous AI Execution Pattern
[codex] 
[codex] The preferred AI execution pattern in this repo is autonomous execution with clear success criteria. For rationale, safety model, Docker access patterns, and examples, see `docs/architecture/autonomous-ai-execution.md`.
[codex] 
[codex] ## SSL/TLS Certificate Constraints
[codex] 
[codex] NEVER run SSL write operations from the AI container.
[codex] 
[codex] The container has its own `mkcert` CA, but the user's browser trusts the host CA. Certificates generated inside the container will cause browser trust failures.
[codex] 
[codex] Forbidden operations that must be run by the user on the host:
[codex] - `mkcert`
[codex] - `openssl genrsa`
[codex] - `openssl req -new`
[codex] - `openssl x509 -req`
[codex] - Any script that generates browser or infrastructure certificates, including `scripts/bootstrap/setup-k8s-tls.sh` and `scripts/bootstrap/setup-infra-tls.sh`
[codex] 
[codex] Allowed read-only operations:
[codex] - `openssl x509 -text -noout`
[codex] - `openssl verify`
[codex] - `kubectl get secret -o yaml`
[codex] - Certificate file reads for debugging
[codex] 
[codex] For live agent work against exactly
[codex] `https://app.budgetanalyzer.localhost`, agents may run the workspace-owned
[codex] `ensure-budget-analyzer-local-ca-trust` command to install the host-published
[codex] public root into container-local trust stores. Use
[codex] `check-budget-analyzer-local-ca-trust` for read-only trust diagnosis. Read
[codex] `../workspace/docs/local-budget-analyzer-tls.md` before changing or debugging
[codex] this flow.
[codex] 
[codex] If `nginx/certs/k8s/_mkcert-rootCA.pem` is missing, invalid, or stale, stop and
[codex] ask the user to run `./setup.sh` from the orchestration checkout on the host.
[codex] Do not generate or rotate certificates in-container, and do not bypass
[codex] verification with HTTP, `--insecure`, `verify=False`, or
[codex] `ignore_https_errors`.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Path Portability
[codex] 
[codex] Never hardcode absolute workspace paths such as `/workspace`.
[codex] 
[codex] Use relative paths or dynamically resolved paths instead:
[codex] - `../service-common`
[codex] - `$(dirname "$0")`
[codex] - Tilt variables such as `config.main_dir`
[codex] 
[codex] All repositories are expected to be cloned side-by-side under a common parent directory so cross-repo relative references continue to work.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Historical Docs
[codex] 
[codex] Ignore `docs/archive/` for active implementation work.
[codex] 
[codex] Use `docs/decisions/` for context and rationale, not as an implementation surface. Do not edit decision records unless explicitly asked.
[codex] 
[codex] ## NO GIT WRITE OPERATIONS
[codex] 
[codex] Never run git write commands such as `commit`, `push`, `checkout`, `reset`, or branch manipulation without explicit user request. The user owns git workflow entirely.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Honest Discourse
[codex] 
[codex] Do not over-validate ideas. The user wants honest pushback, not agreement.
[codex] 
[codex] - If something seems wrong, say so directly
[codex] - Distinguish novel ideas from ideas that are obvious in retrospect
[codex] - Push back on vague claims and ask for concrete constraints
[codex] - Skip empty praise and filler

[codex] # Documentation Ownership
[codex] 
[codex] **Status:** Active
[codex] **Purpose:** Define the canonical owner for recurring documentation topics so
[codex] exact operational detail lives in one place and non-owner docs summarize and
[codex] link instead of copying.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Rules
[codex] 
[codex] - One recurring topic should have one canonical owner.
[codex] - Non-owner docs may give a short summary, orientation paragraph, or diagram,
[codex]   but they should not own the full command sequence, table, or contract.
[codex] - When a topic changes, update the owner doc first, then update summaries,
[codex]   indexes, and cross-links that point to it.
[codex] - `docs/archive/` is historical reference only and does not own active
[codex]   implementation detail.
[codex] - If no existing owner fits a new recurring topic, choose one and update this
[codex]   file in the same change.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Exact Detail vs. Summary
[codex] 
[codex] In this repo, "exact detail" includes things such as:
[codex] 
[codex] - full startup and verifier sequences
[codex] - complete port tables
[codex] - observability access commands
[codex] - detailed route ownership lists
[codex] - detailed `/api-docs` behavior and output definitions
[codex] 
[codex] Those details should live in the owner doc. Non-owner docs can summarize the
[codex] shape of the topic and link outward.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Ownership Map
[codex] 
[codex] | Topic | Canonical owner | Rule for non-owner docs |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | Documentation ownership policy | [docs/OWNERSHIP.md](OWNERSHIP.md) | Link here instead of restating the full ownership map. |
[codex] | AI instructions and repo guardrails | [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md) | Other docs may describe the repo, but agent instructions and repo-operating constraints belong there. |
[codex] | Reusable `AGENTS.md` authoring standard | [docs/agents-md-checkstyle.md](agents-md-checkstyle.md) | Link here instead of restating the pattern-based authoring rules. |
[codex] | Release/deployment operating model and terminology | [docs/ci-cd.md](ci-cd.md) | Deployment docs and runbooks may summarize the modes, but source tags, artifact versions, `service-common` versions, image digests, and desired-state manifest terminology belong there. |
[codex] | OCI release deployment checklist and run log template | [docs/runbooks/oci-release-deployment-checklist.md](runbooks/oci-release-deployment-checklist.md) | Link to the template instead of copying the evidence table into plans or deployment docs. |
[codex] | Dependency automation operating policy | [docs/dependency-automation.md](dependency-automation.md) | Keep activation, cost, bot ownership, and triage detail there; the rollout plan owns implementation phases and the preserved review remains the historical benchmark. |
[codex] | Supported local happy path | [docs/development/getting-started.md](development/getting-started.md) | Point readers there instead of repeating the full supported setup flow. |
[codex] | Local environment mechanics and live-update internals | [docs/development/local-environment.md](development/local-environment.md) | Keep implementation detail there; summarize elsewhere. |
[codex] | Host-published local ingress CA contract | [docs/development/local-environment.md](development/local-environment.md) | Setup and agent docs may name the published file and consumer command, but keep certificate publication, trust ownership, and troubleshooting detail here. |
[codex] | Autonomous agent execution and local credential trust boundary | [docs/architecture/autonomous-ai-execution.md](architecture/autonomous-ai-execution.md) | Plans and setup docs may summarize the boundary, but local-only agent authority, credential exposure assumptions, and production exclusions belong there. |
[codex] | Manual Tilt/Kind bootstrap internals | [docs/tilt-kind-setup-guide.md](tilt-kind-setup-guide.md) | Treat it as a manual/deep-dive reference, not a competing default path. |
[codex] | High-level system orientation | [docs/architecture/system-overview.md](architecture/system-overview.md) | Keep this high level and defer exact contracts to the topic owners below. |
[codex] | Browser request flow and shared session contract | [docs/architecture/session-edge-authorization-pattern.md](architecture/session-edge-authorization-pattern.md) | Other docs may summarize the flow, but detailed route ownership and session contract behavior belong there. |
[codex] | `ext-authz` service implementation and release workflow | [../ext-authz/README.md](../../ext-authz/README.md) | Orchestration docs own deployment, Istio wiring, Redis ACLs, production policy, and cross-cutting session-edge behavior. |
[codex] | Security controls and layered posture | [docs/architecture/security-architecture.md](architecture/security-architecture.md) | Link there for detailed control rationale and layered defenses. |
[codex] | Ports and service exposure | [docs/architecture/port-reference.md](architecture/port-reference.md) | Do not maintain competing port tables elsewhere. |
[codex] | Observability access model and operator entry points | [docs/architecture/observability.md](architecture/observability.md) | Keep exact access posture and port-forward commands there. |
[codex] | Resource-routing authoring and NGINX route work | [nginx/README.md](../nginx/README.md) | Architecture docs may reference the pattern, but NGINX route authoring detail belongs there. |
[codex] | Local script catalog and verifier entry points | [scripts/README.md](../scripts/README.md) | Other docs may call out specific scripts, but the `scripts/` tree map and canonical verifier entry points belong there. |
[codex] | OCI deployment operator surface and deploy script map | [deploy/README.md](../deploy/README.md) | Other docs may summarize OCI bootstrap, render, reconcile, release, and verification commands, but detailed deploy run order and `deploy/scripts/` lifecycle mapping belong there. |
[codex] | Unified API docs surface (`/api-docs`) | [docs-aggregator/README.md](../docs-aggregator/README.md) | Keep exact route behavior, generated outputs, and docs-surface details there. |
[codex] 
[codex] ## `AGENTS.md` Special Case
[codex] 
[codex] `AGENTS.md` is intentionally rich direct AI context. It is the canonical source
[codex] for repo guardrails, execution constraints, and orchestration-specific working
[codex] rules.
[codex] 
[codex] It is not the canonical owner for every exact operational detail it mentions.
[codex] When `AGENTS.md` references setup flows, ports, observability access, routing,
[codex] or `/api-docs` behavior, prefer a stable summary plus a pointer to the owner
[codex] doc above instead of maintaining a second full inventory there.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Maintenance Workflow
[codex] 
[codex] When a recurring topic changes:
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Update the canonical owner doc first.
[codex] 2. Update summaries, indexes, and cross-links that mention the topic.
[codex] 3. Remove or shorten any duplicated exact detail that now conflicts with the
[codex]    owner doc.
[codex] 4. If ownership changed, update this file in the same change.
[codex] # Dependency Update Review Baseline
[codex] 
[codex] **Date:** 2026-09-06  
[codex] **Status:** Point-in-time research  
[codex] **Purpose:** Preserve the manually researched dependency recommendations so
[codex] they can be compared with repository-owned Dependabot and Renovate findings
[codex] after that automation is enabled.
[codex] 
[codex] This is not a version contract or an implementation plan. Upstream current
[codex] versions will continue to change. Checked-in build files, manifests, version
[codex] catalogs, and `deploy/scripts/lib/version-contract.sh` remain the sources of
[codex] truth for selected versions.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Executive Summary
[codex] 
[codex] The highest-priority work is not a blanket migration to every current major.
[codex] It is a security patch batch covering Redis, the Spring-managed dependency
[codex] stack, External Secrets Operator, Kyverno, the frontend dependency tree,
[codex] `go-redis`, the JDK runtime image, and selected observability components.
[codex] 
[codex] Several runtime lines are also unsupported or approaching end of support:
[codex] Spring Boot 3.5, Node.js 20, Go 1.24, RabbitMQ 3.13, NGINX 1.29, and production
[codex] Kubernetes/K3s 1.34. Those should be handled as explicit migration batches
[codex] rather than mixed into low-risk security patch updates.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Security And Maintenance Patch Recommendations
[codex] 
[codex] ### Redis Server
[codex] 
[codex] | Item | Finding |
[codex] | --- | --- |
[codex] | Checked-in reference | `redis:7-alpine@sha256:7aec734b...` |
[codex] | Version resolved from the digest | Redis 7.4.8, image created 2026-04-17 |
[codex] | Recommended supported-line patch | Redis 7.4.11 |
[codex] | Major migration status | Redis 8 exists, but is not required for support |
[codex] 
[codex] Redis 7.4.8 predates the May 2026 security release and is affected by multiple
[codex] authenticated memory-safety vulnerabilities, including a `RESTORE` command
[codex] issue that may lead to remote code execution. This has practical relevance to
[codex] the current ACL model: `session-gateway` and `redis-ops` receive `+@all`, while
[codex] the other service identities are substantially narrower.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: refresh the Redis 7.4 image to 7.4.11 before considering a
[codex] Redis 8 migration. Review whether `session-gateway` needs `+@all` or can use an
[codex] explicit command allowlist that excludes administrative commands such as
[codex] `RESTORE`.
[codex] 
[codex] Primary advisory:
[codex] [GHSA-c8h9-259x-jff4](https://github.com/redis/redis/security/advisories/GHSA-c8h9-259x-jff4).
[codex] 
[codex] ### Spring Boot-Managed Backend Stack
[codex] 
[codex] The checked-in Spring Boot 3.5.14 BOM resolves these notable components:
[codex] 
[codex] | Component | Boot 3.5.14 | Boot 3.5.16 | Security floor observed in this review |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | Spring Framework | 6.2.18 | 6.2.19 | 6.2.19 |
[codex] | Spring Security | 6.5.10 | 6.5.11 | No advisory found for 6.5.10 in the queried database |
[codex] | Jackson BOM | 2.21.2 | 2.21.4 | 2.21.4 |
[codex] | Netty | 4.1.132.Final | 4.1.135.Final | 4.1.137.Final |
[codex] | Embedded Tomcat | 10.1.54 | 10.1.55 | 10.1.58 |
[codex] 
[codex] Findings include:
[codex] 
[codex] - Spring Web 6.2.18 is affected by an `UriComponentsBuilder` SSRF advisory;
[codex]   6.2.19 contains the fix.
[codex] - Jackson 2.21.2 is affected by deserialization authorization and polymorphic
[codex]   type validation issues; the relevant 2.x fixes are present in 2.21.4.
[codex] - Netty 4.1.132 is affected by request-smuggling, decompression denial of
[codex]   service, CORS bypass, CRLF injection, and resource exhaustion advisories.
[codex] - Tomcat 10.1.54 is in the affected ranges for several authorization and
[codex]   authentication advisories. The newest reviewed fixes require 10.1.58.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: first move the coordinated Java repositories to Spring Boot
[codex] 3.5.16. Because its BOM still selects Netty 4.1.135 and Tomcat 10.1.55, assess
[codex] temporary managed-version overrides to Netty 4.1.137 and Tomcat 10.1.58. Keep
[codex] this patch batch separate from the Spring Boot 4 migration.
[codex] 
[codex] Applicability caveat: several Tomcat findings concern container-managed
[codex] FORM/DIGEST authentication, WebDAV, AJP, or direct HTTP/2 behavior that the
[codex] current services do not appear to use. The affected components are still in
[codex] the deployed classpath, so patching remains preferable to relying only on
[codex] configuration non-use. Netty is directly relevant to the reactive Session
[codex] Gateway and other WebFlux/WebClient paths.
[codex] 
[codex] Representative advisories:
[codex] 
[codex] - [Spring Framework SSRF](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-7m2p-62gw-p8qq)
[codex] - [Tomcat security constraint bypass](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-5m62-pw8w-7w9f)
[codex] - [Tomcat authentication bypass](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-9xv2-5v5q-p794)
[codex] - [Netty request smuggling](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-38f8-5428-x5cv)
[codex] - [Netty CORS header issue](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-8c42-7qj2-3j46)
[codex] - [Jackson polymorphic validation bypass](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-j3rv-43j4-c7qm)
[codex] 
[codex] ### External Secrets Operator
[codex] 
[codex] | Item | Finding |
[codex] | --- | --- |
[codex] | Checked-in chart | 2.2.0 |
[codex] | Minimum security target identified | 2.4.0 |
[codex] | Current chart at review time | 2.10.0 |
[codex] 
[codex] External Secrets 2.2.0 is affected by DNS-based secret exfiltration through
[codex] the v2 template engine and a namespace-isolation bypass in `CAProvider`
[codex] ConfigMap resolution. Because this controller handles production secrets and
[codex] has broad cluster authority, it belongs in the critical monitoring tier even
[codex] though it was omitted from the former notification guide.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: validate a direct move to the current 2.10.0 chart. Do not
[codex] remain below 2.4.0.
[codex] 
[codex] Advisories:
[codex] 
[codex] - [GHSA-r2pg-r6h7-crf3](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-r2pg-r6h7-crf3)
[codex] - [GHSA-wv26-88m5-6h59](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-wv26-88m5-6h59)
[codex] 
[codex] ### Kyverno
[codex] 
[codex] | Item | Finding |
[codex] | --- | --- |
[codex] | Checked-in chart/application | 3.8.0 / 1.18.0 |
[codex] | Minimum security target identified | Application 1.18.2 |
[codex] | Current chart/application at review time | 3.9.0 / 1.19.0 |
[codex] 
[codex] Kyverno 1.18.0 and 1.18.1 are affected by a critical cross-namespace resource
[codex] generation vulnerability in namespaced CEL policies. The current repository
[codex] does not define `NamespacedMutatingPolicy` or `NamespacedGeneratingPolicy`
[codex] resources, which reduces present exploitability, but the controller is a
[codex] privileged admission component.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: update the Helm chart and Kyverno CLI together to the 1.19.0
[codex] application line, then render and rerun the existing policy and security
[codex] guardrail tests.
[codex] 
[codex] Advisory:
[codex] [GHSA-79gf-7frw-68m9](https://github.com/kyverno/kyverno/security/advisories/GHSA-79gf-7frw-68m9).
[codex] 
[codex] ### Frontend Dependency Tree
[codex] 
[codex] `npm audit` against the checked-in lockfile reported 20 findings: 3 critical,
[codex] 13 high, 2 moderate, and 2 low. `npm audit --omit=dev` reported four high
[codex] findings associated with Axios, React Router, React Router DOM, and a
[codex] transitive Axios dependency.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommended patch batch:
[codex] 
[codex] | Package | Checked-in/locked baseline | Patch target at review time |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | React / React DOM | 19.2.5 | 19.2.8 |
[codex] | React Router / React Router DOM | 7.14.2 | 7.18.3 |
[codex] | Axios | 1.16.0 | 1.20.0 |
[codex] | Vite | 6.4.2 | 6.4.3 or a separately planned major |
[codex] | Vitest packages | 3.2.4 | 3.2.7 or a separately planned major |
[codex] | PostCSS | 8.5.14 | 8.5.28 |
[codex] 
[codex] Refresh the lockfile after updating the direct dependencies so patched
[codex] versions of `form-data`, `brace-expansion`, `browserslist`, `flatted`,
[codex] `js-yaml`, `nanoid`, `picomatch`, and `ws` are selected.
[codex] 
[codex] Applicability caveats:
[codex] 
[codex] - The critical Vitest issue requires the Vitest UI server to be listening; it
[codex]   is a development-tool risk, not production browser code.
[codex] - The reviewed Vite 6.4.2 issue requires a network-exposed Windows dev server.
[codex]   The supported container path is Linux, but the patch is still low risk.
[codex] - Most React Router findings concern framework, SSR, manifest, or RSC modes.
[codex]   The application uses `BrowserRouter`, not those server modes. The open
[codex]   redirect advisory is the most relevant browser-side reason to update.
[codex] - React Server Component advisories do not apply because no
[codex]   `react-server-dom-*` package is installed. React 19.2.8 remains the sensible
[codex]   paired patch level.
[codex] 
[codex] ### ext-authz And go-redis
[codex] 
[codex] `govulncheck` found a reachable call path from `ext-authz` to GO-2025-3540 in
[codex] `github.com/redis/go-redis/v9` 9.7.0. The issue may cause out-of-order responses
[codex] when `CLIENT SETINFO` times out during connection establishment. The first
[codex] fixed version is 9.7.3; the current release at review time is 9.22.0.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: update within go-redis v9, rerun `go mod tidy`, `go test ./...`,
[codex] `govulncheck ./...`, and the shared session-contract verifiers.
[codex] 
[codex] Advisory: [GO-2025-3540](https://pkg.go.dev/vuln/GO-2025-3540).
[codex] 
[codex] ### JDK Runtime Image
[codex] 
[codex] The pinned `eclipse-temurin:25-jre-alpine` digest resolves to JDK 25.0.3+9,
[codex] created on 2026-04-30. The current Temurin 25 maintenance release is
[codex] 25.0.4.1+1.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: refresh the Temurin digest and verify that the separately
[codex] installed Azul Zulu 25 devcontainer package receives the same quarterly CPU
[codex] level. Java 25 remains the selected LTS baseline; this is not a Java feature
[codex] release migration.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Istio
[codex] 
[codex] | Item | Finding |
[codex] | --- | --- |
[codex] | Checked-in release | 1.29.2 |
[codex] | Latest patch on selected line | 1.29.7 |
[codex] | Current minor at review time | 1.31.0 |
[codex] 
[codex] Istio 1.29.2 is affected by a medium-severity failure mode in which an
[codex] unresolved `BackendTLSPolicy` CA reference can cause sidecars to send upstream
[codex] traffic in plaintext. The repository does not currently define a
[codex] `BackendTLSPolicy`, so the advisory is not presently reachable through the
[codex] checked-in configuration.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: take 1.29.7 as the minimum maintenance update and plan a move
[codex] to 1.30 or 1.31 with the Kubernetes, Gateway API, and Calico batch. Istio 1.29
[codex] is expected to leave support around the end of October 2026.
[codex] 
[codex] Sources:
[codex] 
[codex] - [Istio advisory GHSA-qm8v-g4f9-qhjx](https://github.com/istio/istio/security/advisories/GHSA-qm8v-g4f9-qhjx)
[codex] - [Istio supported releases](https://istio.io/latest/docs/releases/supported-releases/)
[codex] 
[codex] ### Observability
[codex] 
[codex] | Component | Checked-in version | Recommendation |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | Prometheus | 3.11.1 | At least 3.11.3; prefer a currently supported line such as 3.13 |
[codex] | Grafana | 12.4.2 | Refresh to the latest 12.4 patch, 12.4.10 at review time |
[codex] | Prometheus Operator | 0.90.1 via chart 83.4.0 | Review through the chart upgrade |
[codex] | kube-prometheus-stack | 83.4.0 | Current chart was 89.2.2; treat as a rendered-manifest migration |
[codex] | Jaeger | 2.17.0 | Current was 2.20.0; no directly applicable advisory found |
[codex] | Kiali | 2.24.0 | Current chart was 2.31.0; no directly applicable advisory found |
[codex] 
[codex] Prometheus 3.11.1 is the last affected release for a stored XSS issue and is
[codex] also affected by a remote-read denial-of-service issue fixed in 3.11.3. The
[codex] observability services are internal-only and operator access is loopback-only,
[codex] which lowers exposure but does not eliminate the need for patching.
[codex] 
[codex] Sources:
[codex] 
[codex] - [Prometheus stored XSS](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-vffh-x6r8-xx99)
[codex] - [Prometheus remote-read denial of service](https://github.com/prometheus/prometheus/security/advisories/GHSA-8rm2-7qqf-34qm)
[codex] 
[codex] ### PostgreSQL
[codex] 
[codex] The pinned `postgres:16-alpine` digest resolves to PostgreSQL 16.13. The
[codex] current patch at review time is 16.15.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: refresh to PostgreSQL 16.15 as routine security and bug-fix
[codex] maintenance. PostgreSQL 16 remains supported until November 2028, so the
[codex] existence of PostgreSQL 18 does not make a database major-version migration
[codex] urgent.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Unsupported Or Approaching-EOL Migration Batches
[codex] 
[codex] ### Spring 4 Migration
[codex] 
[codex] Spring Boot 3.5.16 was the latest 3.5 release found, and the standard 3.5
[codex] open-source lifecycle ended in June 2026. Current Spring releases at review
[codex] time were Spring Boot 4.1.1, Spring Cloud 2025.1.3, and Spring Security 7.1.1.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: complete the Spring Boot 3.5 security patch batch first, then
[codex] plan the already identified coupled migration to Spring Boot 4, Spring Cloud
[codex] 2025.1, Spring Framework 7, Spring Security 7, Spring Modulith 2, SpringDoc 3,
[codex] Testcontainers 2, ShedLock 7, and JUnit Platform 6.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Node.js
[codex] 
[codex] The frontend Dockerfiles use Node 20, which reached end of life on 2026-04-30.
[codex] The devcontainer already selects Node 22 even though the orchestration docs
[codex] still say Node 20.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: move production and development frontend builds to Node 24
[codex] LTS. Do not target Node 26 until it enters LTS unless there is a deliberate
[codex] reason to follow the current feature line.
[codex] 
[codex] Source: [Node.js releases](https://nodejs.org/en/about/previous-releases).
[codex] 
[codex] ### Go
[codex] 
[codex] Go 1.24 is outside the supported two-release window. Go 1.27.1 was current at
[codex] review time, with Go 1.26 also supported.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: choose Go 1.26 for the conservative baseline or Go 1.27 for
[codex] the current baseline, and update `ext-authz`, its builder image, and the
[codex] devcontainer together. The devcontainer download is also hardcoded to the
[codex] amd64 archive and should be made architecture-aware as part of that work.
[codex] 
[codex] Source: [Go release policy](https://go.dev/doc/devel/release#policy).
[codex] 
[codex] ### RabbitMQ
[codex] 
[codex] The pinned digest resolves to RabbitMQ 3.13.7. RabbitMQ 3.13 has been out of
[codex] community support since September 2024; RabbitMQ 4.3.5 was current at review
[codex] time.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: create a dedicated stateful migration plan to RabbitMQ 4.3,
[codex] covering supported upgrade hops, feature flags, persisted data compatibility,
[codex] definitions import, Spring Cloud Stream compatibility, rollback, and backup
[codex] evidence.
[codex] 
[codex] Source: [RabbitMQ release information](https://www.rabbitmq.com/release-information).
[codex] 
[codex] ### NGINX
[codex] 
[codex] The edge gateway uses NGINX 1.29.4, and the 1.29 mainline is no longer
[codex] supported. NGINX 1.30.4 was the stable line and 1.31.5 the mainline release at
[codex] review time.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: select either stable 1.30.4 or mainline 1.31.5 and update the
[codex] unprivileged image, configuration validation, security documentation, and
[codex] routing smoke tests together.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Kubernetes, K3s, And Local Kind
[codex] 
[codex] | Component | Checked-in version | Review-time status |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | Production K3s | 1.34.6+k3s1 | 1.34.11+k3s1 available; 1.34 EOL expected 2026-10-27 |
[codex] | Stable K3s channel | N/A | 1.36.4+k3s1 |
[codex] | Local Kind node | Kubernetes 1.35.0 | 1.35.8 current patch; Kubernetes 1.37 current minor |
[codex] | Kind binary | 0.31.0 | 0.33.0 current |
[codex] | Gateway API | 1.5.1 | 1.6.2 current |
[codex] | Calico | 3.32.0 | 3.32.2 current |
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: patch K3s 1.34 immediately if the minor migration is not
[codex] ready, then select a parity target—likely Kubernetes/K3s 1.36—compatible with
[codex] Istio 1.31, Gateway API, Calico, kubectl, Kind, Pod Security version labels,
[codex] and the production upgrade process.
[codex] 
[codex] Sources:
[codex] 
[codex] - [K3s release channels](https://update.k3s.io/v1-release/channels)
[codex] - [Kubernetes releases](https://kubernetes.io/releases/)
[codex] - [Istio supported Kubernetes versions](https://istio.io/latest/docs/releases/supported-releases/)
[codex] 
[codex] ## Important Major Releases Without Immediate Security Urgency
[codex] 
[codex] These should appear in an automated dependency dashboard, but should not
[codex] displace the security and EOL work above:
[codex] 
[codex] | Area | Major release available at review time | Disposition |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | PostgreSQL | 18.6 | Defer; PostgreSQL 16 remains supported |
[codex] | Redis | 8.10.1 | Defer until after the Redis 7.4 security patch |
[codex] | Helm | 4.2.4 | Deliberate compatibility project; Helm 4 is currently blocked |
[codex] | Testcontainers | 2.0.5 | Include with the Spring 4/test-stack migration |
[codex] | TypeScript | 7.0.2 | Separate frontend toolchain migration |
[codex] | Vite | 8.2.2 | Separate frontend toolchain migration |
[codex] | Vitest | 5.0.0 | Keep Vitest packages in lockstep |
[codex] | React Router | 8.3.1 | Patch v7 first; evaluate v8 separately |
[codex] | Tailwind CSS | 4.3.3 | Separate styling/build migration with CSP validation |
[codex] | ESLint | 10.10.0 | Separate lint configuration migration |
[codex] 
[codex] React remains on major 19. React Server Component advisories should not be
[codex] reported as applicable to the current SPA unless a `react-server-dom-*`
[codex] dependency or an RSC-capable server framework is introduced.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Documentation Drift Found During The Review
[codex] 
[codex] The former dependency notification guide is unsuitable as the primary alert
[codex] mechanism, but it also contains concrete drift worth preserving before it is
[codex] archived:
[codex] 
[codex] - It says no known issues are tracked, which is no longer true.
[codex] - It omits K3s, External Secrets Operator, cert-manager, the Prometheus stack,
[codex]   Grafana, Prometheus, Jaeger, Kiali, Swagger UI, and other deployed images
[codex]   from its supposedly complete inventory.
[codex] - It records only human-readable image tags, hiding the exact patch version
[codex]   frozen behind each digest.
[codex] - It says Node 20 will reach EOL in April 2026; that date has passed.
[codex] - It treats Go 1.24 and RabbitMQ 3.13 as moderate-priority maintained lines;
[codex]   both are unsupported.
[codex] - It says Redis 7 is the current major and refers to PostgreSQL 17 as the next
[codex]   major; Redis 8 and PostgreSQL 18 are current.
[codex] - It says Helm 4 is only a future GA timeline to watch; Helm 4 is already GA.
[codex] - Its Istio support description uses an N/N-1 rule. Upstream currently defines
[codex]   support until approximately six weeks after N+2.
[codex] - Its Vite summary says 6.0.1 while the inventory and package manifest say
[codex]   6.4.2.
[codex] - It calls Motion "Framer Motion" 11.11.17 while the frontend now depends on
[codex]   the `motion` package at 13.1.0.
[codex] - `docs/development/devcontainer-installed-software.md` says Node 20 although
[codex]   the sibling Dockerfile selects Node 22, and says cert-manager 1.13.2 while
[codex]   the production version contract selects 1.20.2.
[codex] 
[codex] When dependency automation is implemented, update the canonical owner first,
[codex] then synchronize `docs/development/local-environment.md`,
[codex] `docs/development/devcontainer-installed-software.md`,
[codex] `docs/architecture/security-architecture.md`,
[codex] `docs/architecture/observability.md`, and `deploy/README.md` only where their
[codex] owned behavior or exact operational contract changes. Do not rewrite ADRs to
[codex] reflect current versions.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Automation Comparison Baseline
[codex] 
[codex] No checked-in Dependabot or Renovate configuration was found in the reviewed
[codex] Budget Analyzer repositories. Repository settings such as Dependabot alerts
[codex] could not be inferred from the checkout.
[codex] 
[codex] After automation is enabled, compare its initial dashboard and pull requests
[codex] against this research using these categories:
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Security findings reproduced exactly.
[codex] 2. Security findings omitted by automation, especially Helm-managed
[codex]    applications and digest-pinned Kubernetes images.
[codex] 3. Findings automation raises that are configuration-specific or unreachable.
[codex] 4. Supported-line patch updates versus major migrations.
[codex] 5. EOL findings that dependency version tools do not normally report.
[codex] 6. Cross-repository coupling groups that must be merged and verified together.
[codex] 
[codex] Expected high-signal matches are Redis, Spring-managed transitive libraries,
[codex] External Secrets Operator, Kyverno, frontend direct and transitive packages,
[codex] `go-redis`, Temurin, Istio, Prometheus/Grafana, and the Kubernetes toolchain.
[codex]

[codex] #!/usr/bin/env bash
[codex] 
[codex] # Shared version contract for the Oracle Cloud production install path.
[codex] # Source this file from deploy/scripts/*.sh instead of duplicating version pins.
[codex] 
[codex] export PHASE4_VERSION_CONTRACT_EFFECTIVE_DATE="2026-04-15"
[codex] readonly PHASE4_VERSION_CONTRACT_EFFECTIVE_DATE
[codex] 
[codex] # Selection rule:
[codex] # - prefer the upstream stable or explicitly supported release line
[codex] # - then take the latest patch in that line
[codex] # - do not automatically follow a project's latest channel or a newly
[codex] #   published minor release
[codex] 
[codex] # renovate: datasource=github-releases depName=k3s-io/k3s versioning=regex:^v(?&lt;major>\d+)\.(?&lt;minor>\d+)\.(?&lt;patch>\d+)\+k3s(?&lt;build>\d+)$
[codex] export PHASE4_K3S_VERSION="v1.34.6+k3s1"
[codex] readonly PHASE4_K3S_VERSION
[codex] export PHASE4_K3S_INSTALL_URL="https://get.k3s.io"
[codex] readonly PHASE4_K3S_INSTALL_URL
[codex] export PHASE4_MIN_INOTIFY_INSTANCES="8192"
[codex] readonly PHASE4_MIN_INOTIFY_INSTANCES
[codex] export PHASE4_MIN_INOTIFY_WATCHES="524288"
[codex] readonly PHASE4_MIN_INOTIFY_WATCHES
[codex] # renovate: datasource=github-releases depName=kubernetes-sigs/gateway-api
[codex] export PHASE4_GATEWAY_API_CRDS_VERSION="v1.5.1"
[codex] readonly PHASE4_GATEWAY_API_CRDS_VERSION
[codex] export PHASE4_GATEWAY_API_STANDARD_INSTALL_URL="https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/gateway-api/releases/download/${PHASE4_GATEWAY_API_CRDS_VERSION}/standard-install.yaml"

[codex] phase7_normalize_os() {
[codex]     local raw_os
[codex] 
[codex]     raw_os="${1:-$(uname -s)}"
[codex]     case "$raw_os" in
[codex]         Linux|linux)
[codex]             printf 'linux\n'
[codex]             ;;
[codex]         Darwin|darwin)
[codex]             printf 'darwin\n'
[codex]             ;;
[codex]         *)
[codex]             echo "Unsupported operating system: $raw_os" >&2
[codex]             return 1
[codex]             ;;
[codex]     esac
[codex] }
[codex] 
[codex] phase7_normalize_arch() {
[codex]     local raw_arch
[codex] 
[codex]     raw_arch="${1:-$(uname -m)}"
[codex]     case "$raw_arch" in
[codex]         x86_64|amd64)
[codex]             printf 'amd64\n'
[codex]             ;;
[codex]         arm64|aarch64)
[codex]             printf 'arm64\n'
[codex]             ;;
[codex]         *)
[codex]             echo "Unsupported CPU architecture: $raw_arch" >&2
[codex]             return 1
[codex]             ;;
[codex]     esac
[codex] }
[codex] 
[codex] phase7_platform_key() {
[codex]     local os
[codex]     local arch
[codex] 
[codex]     os="$(phase7_normalize_os "${1:-}")" || return 1
[codex]     arch="$(phase7_normalize_arch "${2:-}")" || return 1
[codex]     printf '%s-%s\n' "$os" "$arch"
[codex] }
[codex] 
[codex] phase7_sha256_file() {
[codex]     if command -v sha256sum >/dev/null 2>&1; then
[codex]         sha256sum "$1" | awk '{print $1}'
[codex]         return 0
[codex]     fi
[codex] 
[codex]     if command -v shasum >/dev/null 2>&1; then
[codex]         shasum -a 256 "$1" | awk '{print $1}'
[codex]         return 0
[codex]     fi
[codex] 
[codex]     echo "Neither sha256sum nor shasum is available" >&2
[codex]     return 1
[codex] }
[codex] 
[codex] phase7_verify_sha256() {
[codex]     local file expected actual
[codex] 
[codex]     file="$1"
[codex]     expected="$2"
[codex]     actual="$(phase7_sha256_file "$file")" || return 1
[codex] 
[codex]     if [[ "$actual" != "$expected" ]]; then
[codex]         echo "Checksum mismatch for $file" >&2
[codex]         echo "Expected: $expected" >&2
[codex]         echo "Actual:   $actual" >&2
[codex]         return 1
[codex]     fi
[codex] }
[codex]

[codex] phase7_install_hint() {
[codex]     local tool repo_root installer_path
[codex] 
[codex]     tool="$1"
[codex]     repo_root="${2:-.}"
[codex]     installer_path="${repo_root%/}/scripts/bootstrap/install-verified-tool.sh"
[codex] 
[codex]     case "$tool" in
[codex]         mkcert)
[codex]             printf 'sudo apt-get install -y libnss3-tools && "%s" mkcert\n' "$installer_path"
[codex]             ;;
[codex]         kubectl|helm|tilt|kind)
[codex]             printf '"%s" %s\n' "$installer_path" "$tool"
[codex]             ;;
[codex]         kubeconform|kube-linter|kyverno)
[codex]             printf '"%s" %s\n' "$installer_path" "$tool"
[codex]             ;;
[codex]         *)
[codex]             echo "Unsupported tool hint: $tool" >&2
[codex]             return 1
[codex]             ;;
[codex]     esac
[codex] }

[codex] # Dependency Update Review Baseline
[codex] 
[codex] **Date:** 2026-09-06  
[codex] **Status:** Point-in-time research  
[codex] **Purpose:** Preserve the manually researched dependency recommendations so
[codex] they can be compared with repository-owned Dependabot and Renovate findings
[codex] after that automation is enabled.
[codex] 
[codex] This is not a version contract or an implementation plan. Upstream current
[codex] versions will continue to change. Checked-in build files, manifests, version
[codex] catalogs, and `deploy/scripts/lib/version-contract.sh` remain the sources of
[codex] truth for selected versions.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Executive Summary
[codex] 
[codex] The highest-priority work is not a blanket migration to every current major.
[codex] It is a security patch batch covering Redis, the Spring-managed dependency
[codex] stack, External Secrets Operator, Kyverno, the frontend dependency tree,
[codex] `go-redis`, the JDK runtime image, and selected observability components.
[codex] 
[codex] Several runtime lines are also unsupported or approaching end of support:
[codex] Spring Boot 3.5, Node.js 20, Go 1.24, RabbitMQ 3.13, NGINX 1.29, and production
[codex] Kubernetes/K3s 1.34. Those should be handled as explicit migration batches
[codex] rather than mixed into low-risk security patch updates.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Security And Maintenance Patch Recommendations
[codex] 
[codex] ### Redis Server
[codex] 
[codex] | Item | Finding |
[codex] | --- | --- |
[codex] | Checked-in reference | `redis:7-alpine@sha256:7aec734b...` |
[codex] | Version resolved from the digest | Redis 7.4.8, image created 2026-04-17 |
[codex] | Recommended supported-line patch | Redis 7.4.11 |
[codex] | Major migration status | Redis 8 exists, but is not required for support |
[codex] 
[codex] Redis 7.4.8 predates the May 2026 security release and is affected by multiple
[codex] authenticated memory-safety vulnerabilities, including a `RESTORE` command
[codex] issue that may lead to remote code execution. This has practical relevance to
[codex] the current ACL model: `session-gateway` and `redis-ops` receive `+@all`, while
[codex] the other service identities are substantially narrower.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: refresh the Redis 7.4 image to 7.4.11 before considering a
[codex] Redis 8 migration. Review whether `session-gateway` needs `+@all` or can use an
[codex] explicit command allowlist that excludes administrative commands such as
[codex] `RESTORE`.
[codex] 
[codex] Primary advisory:
[codex] [GHSA-c8h9-259x-jff4](https://github.com/redis/redis/security/advisories/GHSA-c8h9-259x-jff4).
[codex] 
[codex] ### Spring Boot-Managed Backend Stack
[codex] 
[codex] The checked-in Spring Boot 3.5.14 BOM resolves these notable components:
[codex] 
[codex] | Component | Boot 3.5.14 | Boot 3.5.16 | Security floor observed in this review |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | Spring Framework | 6.2.18 | 6.2.19 | 6.2.19 |
[codex] | Spring Security | 6.5.10 | 6.5.11 | No advisory found for 6.5.10 in the queried database |
[codex] | Jackson BOM | 2.21.2 | 2.21.4 | 2.21.4 |
[codex] | Netty | 4.1.132.Final | 4.1.135.Final | 4.1.137.Final |
[codex] | Embedded Tomcat | 10.1.54 | 10.1.55 | 10.1.58 |
[codex] 
[codex] Findings include:
[codex] 
[codex] - Spring Web 6.2.18 is affected by an `UriComponentsBuilder` SSRF advisory;
[codex]   6.2.19 contains the fix.
[codex] - Jackson 2.21.2 is affected by deserialization authorization and polymorphic
[codex]   type validation issues; the relevant 2.x fixes are present in 2.21.4.
[codex] - Netty 4.1.132 is affected by request-smuggling, decompression denial of
[codex]   service, CORS bypass, CRLF injection, and resource exhaustion advisories.
[codex] - Tomcat 10.1.54 is in the affected ranges for several authorization and
[codex]   authentication advisories. The newest reviewed fixes require 10.1.58.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: first move the coordinated Java repositories to Spring Boot
[codex] 3.5.16. Because its BOM still selects Netty 4.1.135 and Tomcat 10.1.55, assess
[codex] temporary managed-version overrides to Netty 4.1.137 and Tomcat 10.1.58. Keep
[codex] this patch batch separate from the Spring Boot 4 migration.
[codex] 
[codex] Applicability caveat: several Tomcat findings concern container-managed
[codex] FORM/DIGEST authentication, WebDAV, AJP, or direct HTTP/2 behavior that the
[codex] current services do not appear to use. The affected components are still in
[codex] the deployed classpath, so patching remains preferable to relying only on
[codex] configuration non-use. Netty is directly relevant to the reactive Session
[codex] Gateway and other WebFlux/WebClient paths.
[codex] 
[codex] Representative advisories:
[codex] 
[codex] - [Spring Framework SSRF](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-7m2p-62gw-p8qq)
[codex] - [Tomcat security constraint bypass](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-5m62-pw8w-7w9f)
[codex] - [Tomcat authentication bypass](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-9xv2-5v5q-p794)
[codex] - [Netty request smuggling](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-38f8-5428-x5cv)
[codex] - [Netty CORS header issue](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-8c42-7qj2-3j46)
[codex] - [Jackson polymorphic validation bypass](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-j3rv-43j4-c7qm)
[codex] 
[codex] ### External Secrets Operator
[codex] 
[codex] | Item | Finding |
[codex] | --- | --- |
[codex] | Checked-in chart | 2.2.0 |
[codex] | Minimum security target identified | 2.4.0 |
[codex] | Current chart at review time | 2.10.0 |
[codex] 
[codex] External Secrets 2.2.0 is affected by DNS-based secret exfiltration through
[codex] the v2 template engine and a namespace-isolation bypass in `CAProvider`
[codex] ConfigMap resolution. Because this controller handles production secrets and
[codex] has broad cluster authority, it belongs in the critical monitoring tier even
[codex] though it was omitted from the former notification guide.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: validate a direct move to the current 2.10.0 chart. Do not
[codex] remain below 2.4.0.
[codex] 
[codex] Advisories:
[codex] 
[codex] - [GHSA-r2pg-r6h7-crf3](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-r2pg-r6h7-crf3)
[codex] - [GHSA-wv26-88m5-6h59](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-wv26-88m5-6h59)
[codex] 
[codex] ### Kyverno
[codex] 
[codex] | Item | Finding |
[codex] | --- | --- |
[codex] | Checked-in chart/application | 3.8.0 / 1.18.0 |
[codex] | Minimum security target identified | Application 1.18.2 |
[codex] | Current chart/application at review time | 3.9.0 / 1.19.0 |
[codex] 
[codex] Kyverno 1.18.0 and 1.18.1 are affected by a critical cross-namespace resource
[codex] generation vulnerability in namespaced CEL policies. The current repository
[codex] does not define `NamespacedMutatingPolicy` or `NamespacedGeneratingPolicy`
[codex] resources, which reduces present exploitability, but the controller is a
[codex] privileged admission component.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: update the Helm chart and Kyverno CLI together to the 1.19.0
[codex] application line, then render and rerun the existing policy and security
[codex] guardrail tests.
[codex] 
[codex] Advisory:
[codex] [GHSA-79gf-7frw-68m9](https://github.com/kyverno/kyverno/security/advisories/GHSA-79gf-7frw-68m9).
[codex] 
[codex] ### Frontend Dependency Tree
[codex] 
[codex] `npm audit` against the checked-in lockfile reported 20 findings: 3 critical,
[codex] 13 high, 2 moderate, and 2 low. `npm audit --omit=dev` reported four high
[codex] findings associated with Axios, React Router, React Router DOM, and a
[codex] transitive Axios dependency.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommended patch batch:
[codex] 
[codex] | Package | Checked-in/locked baseline | Patch target at review time |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | React / React DOM | 19.2.5 | 19.2.8 |
[codex] | React Router / React Router DOM | 7.14.2 | 7.18.3 |
[codex] | Axios | 1.16.0 | 1.20.0 |
[codex] | Vite | 6.4.2 | 6.4.3 or a separately planned major |
[codex] | Vitest packages | 3.2.4 | 3.2.7 or a separately planned major |
[codex] | PostCSS | 8.5.14 | 8.5.28 |
[codex] 
[codex] Refresh the lockfile after updating the direct dependencies so patched
[codex] versions of `form-data`, `brace-expansion`, `browserslist`, `flatted`,
[codex] `js-yaml`, `nanoid`, `picomatch`, and `ws` are selected.
[codex] 
[codex] Applicability caveats:
[codex] 
[codex] - The critical Vitest issue requires the Vitest UI server to be listening; it
[codex]   is a development-tool risk, not production browser code.
[codex] - The reviewed Vite 6.4.2 issue requires a network-exposed Windows dev server.
[codex]   The supported container path is Linux, but the patch is still low risk.
[codex] - Most React Router findings concern framework, SSR, manifest, or RSC modes.
[codex]   The application uses `BrowserRouter`, not those server modes. The open
[codex]   redirect advisory is the most relevant browser-side reason to update.
[codex] - React Server Component advisories do not apply because no
[codex]   `react-server-dom-*` package is installed. React 19.2.8 remains the sensible
[codex]   paired patch level.
[codex] 
[codex] ### ext-authz And go-redis
[codex] 
[codex] `govulncheck` found a reachable call path from `ext-authz` to GO-2025-3540 in
[codex] `github.com/redis/go-redis/v9` 9.7.0. The issue may cause out-of-order responses
[codex] when `CLIENT SETINFO` times out during connection establishment. The first
[codex] fixed version is 9.7.3; the current release at review time is 9.22.0.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: update within go-redis v9, rerun `go mod tidy`, `go test ./...`,
[codex] `govulncheck ./...`, and the shared session-contract verifiers.
[codex] 
[codex] Advisory: [GO-2025-3540](https://pkg.go.dev/vuln/GO-2025-3540).
[codex] 
[codex] ### JDK Runtime Image
[codex] 
[codex] The pinned `eclipse-temurin:25-jre-alpine` digest resolves to JDK 25.0.3+9,
[codex] created on 2026-04-30. The current Temurin 25 maintenance release is
[codex] 25.0.4.1+1.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: refresh the Temurin digest and verify that the separately
[codex] installed Azul Zulu 25 devcontainer package receives the same quarterly CPU
[codex] level. Java 25 remains the selected LTS baseline; this is not a Java feature
[codex] release migration.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Istio
[codex] 
[codex] | Item | Finding |
[codex] | --- | --- |
[codex] | Checked-in release | 1.29.2 |
[codex] | Latest patch on selected line | 1.29.7 |
[codex] | Current minor at review time | 1.31.0 |
[codex] 
[codex] Istio 1.29.2 is affected by a medium-severity failure mode in which an
[codex] unresolved `BackendTLSPolicy` CA reference can cause sidecars to send upstream
[codex] traffic in plaintext. The repository does not currently define a
[codex] `BackendTLSPolicy`, so the advisory is not presently reachable through the
[codex] checked-in configuration.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: take 1.29.7 as the minimum maintenance update and plan a move
[codex] to 1.30 or 1.31 with the Kubernetes, Gateway API, and Calico batch. Istio 1.29
[codex] is expected to leave support around the end of October 2026.
[codex] 
[codex] Sources:
[codex] 
[codex] - [Istio advisory GHSA-qm8v-g4f9-qhjx](https://github.com/istio/istio/security/advisories/GHSA-qm8v-g4f9-qhjx)
[codex] - [Istio supported releases](https://istio.io/latest/docs/releases/supported-releases/)
[codex] 
[codex] ### Observability
[codex] 
[codex] | Component | Checked-in version | Recommendation |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | Prometheus | 3.11.1 | At least 3.11.3; prefer a currently supported line such as 3.13 |
[codex] | Grafana | 12.4.2 | Refresh to the latest 12.4 patch, 12.4.10 at review time |
[codex] | Prometheus Operator | 0.90.1 via chart 83.4.0 | Review through the chart upgrade |
[codex] | kube-prometheus-stack | 83.4.0 | Current chart was 89.2.2; treat as a rendered-manifest migration |
[codex] | Jaeger | 2.17.0 | Current was 2.20.0; no directly applicable advisory found |
[codex] | Kiali | 2.24.0 | Current chart was 2.31.0; no directly applicable advisory found |
[codex] 
[codex] Prometheus 3.11.1 is the last affected release for a stored XSS issue and is
[codex] also affected by a remote-read denial-of-service issue fixed in 3.11.3. The
[codex] observability services are internal-only and operator access is loopback-only,
[codex] which lowers exposure but does not eliminate the need for patching.
[codex] 
[codex] Sources:

[codex] ### PostgreSQL
[codex] 
[codex] The pinned `postgres:16-alpine` digest resolves to PostgreSQL 16.13. The
[codex] current patch at review time is 16.15.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: refresh to PostgreSQL 16.15 as routine security and bug-fix
[codex] maintenance. PostgreSQL 16 remains supported until November 2028, so the
[codex] existence of PostgreSQL 18 does not make a database major-version migration
[codex] urgent.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Unsupported Or Approaching-EOL Migration Batches
[codex] 
[codex] ### Spring 4 Migration
[codex] 
[codex] Spring Boot 3.5.16 was the latest 3.5 release found, and the standard 3.5
[codex] open-source lifecycle ended in June 2026. Current Spring releases at review
[codex] time were Spring Boot 4.1.1, Spring Cloud 2025.1.3, and Spring Security 7.1.1.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: complete the Spring Boot 3.5 security patch batch first, then
[codex] plan the already identified coupled migration to Spring Boot 4, Spring Cloud
[codex] 2025.1, Spring Framework 7, Spring Security 7, Spring Modulith 2, SpringDoc 3,
[codex] Testcontainers 2, ShedLock 7, and JUnit Platform 6.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Node.js
[codex] 
[codex] The frontend Dockerfiles use Node 20, which reached end of life on 2026-04-30.
[codex] The devcontainer already selects Node 22 even though the orchestration docs
[codex] still say Node 20.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: move production and development frontend builds to Node 24
[codex] LTS. Do not target Node 26 until it enters LTS unless there is a deliberate
[codex] reason to follow the current feature line.
[codex] 
[codex] Source: [Node.js releases](https://nodejs.org/en/about/previous-releases).
[codex] 
[codex] ### Go
[codex] 
[codex] Go 1.24 is outside the supported two-release window. Go 1.27.1 was current at
[codex] review time, with Go 1.26 also supported.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: choose Go 1.26 for the conservative baseline or Go 1.27 for
[codex] the current baseline, and update `ext-authz`, its builder image, and the
[codex] devcontainer together. The devcontainer download is also hardcoded to the
[codex] amd64 archive and should be made architecture-aware as part of that work.
[codex] 
[codex] Source: [Go release policy](https://go.dev/doc/devel/release#policy).
[codex] 
[codex] ### RabbitMQ
[codex] 
[codex] The pinned digest resolves to RabbitMQ 3.13.7. RabbitMQ 3.13 has been out of
[codex] community support since September 2024; RabbitMQ 4.3.5 was current at review
[codex] time.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: create a dedicated stateful migration plan to RabbitMQ 4.3,
[codex] covering supported upgrade hops, feature flags, persisted data compatibility,
[codex] definitions import, Spring Cloud Stream compatibility, rollback, and backup
[codex] evidence.
[codex] 
[codex] Source: [RabbitMQ release information](https://www.rabbitmq.com/release-information).
[codex] 
[codex] ### NGINX
[codex] 
[codex] The edge gateway uses NGINX 1.29.4, and the 1.29 mainline is no longer
[codex] supported. NGINX 1.30.4 was the stable line and 1.31.5 the mainline release at
[codex] review time.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: select either stable 1.30.4 or mainline 1.31.5 and update the
[codex] unprivileged image, configuration validation, security documentation, and
[codex] routing smoke tests together.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Kubernetes, K3s, And Local Kind
[codex] 
[codex] | Component | Checked-in version | Review-time status |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | Production K3s | 1.34.6+k3s1 | 1.34.11+k3s1 available; 1.34 EOL expected 2026-10-27 |
[codex] | Stable K3s channel | N/A | 1.36.4+k3s1 |
[codex] | Local Kind node | Kubernetes 1.35.0 | 1.35.8 current patch; Kubernetes 1.37 current minor |
[codex] | Kind binary | 0.31.0 | 0.33.0 current |
[codex] | Gateway API | 1.5.1 | 1.6.2 current |
[codex] | Calico | 3.32.0 | 3.32.2 current |
[codex] 
[codex] Recommendation: patch K3s 1.34 immediately if the minor migration is not
[codex] ready, then select a parity target—likely Kubernetes/K3s 1.36—compatible with
[codex] Istio 1.31, Gateway API, Calico, kubectl, Kind, Pod Security version labels,
[codex] and the production upgrade process.
[codex] 
[codex] Sources:
[codex] 
[codex] - [K3s release channels](https://update.k3s.io/v1-release/channels)
[codex] - [Kubernetes releases](https://kubernetes.io/releases/)
[codex] - [Istio supported Kubernetes versions](https://istio.io/latest/docs/releases/supported-releases/)
[codex] 
[codex] ## Important Major Releases Without Immediate Security Urgency
[codex] 
[codex] These should appear in an automated dependency dashboard, but should not
[codex] displace the security and EOL work above:
[codex] 
[codex] | Area | Major release available at review time | Disposition |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | PostgreSQL | 18.6 | Defer; PostgreSQL 16 remains supported |
[codex] | Redis | 8.10.1 | Defer until after the Redis 7.4 security patch |
[codex] | Helm | 4.2.4 | Deliberate compatibility project; Helm 4 is currently blocked |
[codex] | Testcontainers | 2.0.5 | Include with the Spring 4/test-stack migration |
[codex] | TypeScript | 7.0.2 | Separate frontend toolchain migration |
[codex] | Vite | 8.2.2 | Separate frontend toolchain migration |
[codex] | Vitest | 5.0.0 | Keep Vitest packages in lockstep |
[codex] | React Router | 8.3.1 | Patch v7 first; evaluate v8 separately |
[codex] | Tailwind CSS | 4.3.3 | Separate styling/build migration with CSP validation |
[codex] | ESLint | 10.10.0 | Separate lint configuration migration |
[codex] 
[codex] React remains on major 19. React Server Component advisories should not be
[codex] reported as applicable to the current SPA unless a `react-server-dom-*`
[codex] dependency or an RSC-capable server framework is introduced.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Documentation Drift Found During The Review
[codex] 
[codex] The former dependency notification guide is unsuitable as the primary alert
[codex] mechanism, but it also contains concrete drift worth preserving before it is
[codex] archived:
[codex] 
[codex] - It says no known issues are tracked, which is no longer true.
[codex] - It omits K3s, External Secrets Operator, cert-manager, the Prometheus stack,
[codex]   Grafana, Prometheus, Jaeger, Kiali, Swagger UI, and other deployed images
[codex]   from its supposedly complete inventory.
[codex] - It records only human-readable image tags, hiding the exact patch version
[codex]   frozen behind each digest.
[codex] - It says Node 20 will reach EOL in April 2026; that date has passed.
[codex] - It treats Go 1.24 and RabbitMQ 3.13 as moderate-priority maintained lines;
[codex]   both are unsupported.
[codex] - It says Redis 7 is the current major and refers to PostgreSQL 17 as the next
[codex]   major; Redis 8 and PostgreSQL 18 are current.
[codex] - It says Helm 4 is only a future GA timeline to watch; Helm 4 is already GA.
[codex] - Its Istio support description uses an N/N-1 rule. Upstream currently defines
[codex]   support until approximately six weeks after N+2.
[codex] - Its Vite summary says 6.0.1 while the inventory and package manifest say
[codex]   6.4.2.
[codex] - It calls Motion "Framer Motion" 11.11.17 while the frontend now depends on
[codex]   the `motion` package at 13.1.0.
[codex] - `docs/development/devcontainer-installed-software.md` says Node 20 although
[codex]   the sibling Dockerfile selects Node 22, and says cert-manager 1.13.2 while
[codex]   the production version contract selects 1.20.2.
[codex] 
[codex] When dependency automation is implemented, update the canonical owner first,
[codex] then synchronize `docs/development/local-environment.md`,
[codex] `docs/development/devcontainer-installed-software.md`,
[codex] `docs/architecture/security-architecture.md`,
[codex] `docs/architecture/observability.md`, and `deploy/README.md` only where their
[codex] owned behavior or exact operational contract changes. Do not rewrite ADRs to
[codex] reflect current versions.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Automation Comparison Baseline
[codex] 
[codex] No checked-in Dependabot or Renovate configuration was found in the reviewed
[codex] Budget Analyzer repositories. Repository settings such as Dependabot alerts
[codex] could not be inferred from the checkout.
[codex] 
[codex] After automation is enabled, compare its initial dashboard and pull requests
[codex] against this research using these categories:
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Security findings reproduced exactly.
[codex] 2. Security findings omitted by automation, especially Helm-managed
[codex]    applications and digest-pinned Kubernetes images.
[codex] 3. Findings automation raises that are configuration-specific or unreachable.
[codex] 4. Supported-line patch updates versus major migrations.
[codex] 5. EOL findings that dependency version tools do not normally report.
[codex] 6. Cross-repository coupling groups that must be merged and verified together.
[codex] 
[codex] Expected high-signal matches are Redis, Spring-managed transitive libraries,
[codex] External Secrets Operator, Kyverno, frontend direct and transitive packages,
[codex] `go-redis`, Temurin, Istio, Prometheus/Grafana, and the Kubernetes toolchain.
[codex]
